      "charges for the telephone and 50% of the calls. This is because Winston also uses the phone for "
      "personal calls, and 50% of the calls made are personal. The DM decides that 50% of the amount paid by "
      "the employer for the standing and rental charges is an expense wholly, exclusively and necessarily "
      "incurred. The remaining 50% is for Winston’s personal use and so is earnings. The amount paid by the "
      "employer for calls is wholly, exclusively and necessarily incurred and is not earnings."

    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      Span (inline)
        "Example 1 "
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      "A cleaner at a university residential hostel, received 2 weeks holiday pay at the beginning of the 6 weeks "
      "university vacation. On resuming work at the end of the vacation she received a payment of £40, "
      "described by the employer as a retaining fee, at the rate of £10 a week for the 4 weeks in which her "
      "services were not required following the 2 weeks of paid holiday. It was held that the claimant was not "
      "gainfully occupied in an employment since the £40 was not paid by way of wages or reward for services "
      "rendered during the 4 weeks, nor was it paid in consideration of the claimant agreeing to be available to "
      "serve if required, but as an inducement to resume the employment."
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      Span (inline)
        "Example 2 "
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      "A person employed in a school meals service was paid a retaining fee in respect of the school holiday of "
      "six weeks. The payment was made in two equal instalments; the first at the beginning and the second the "
      "day after the end of the school holidays. To be entitled to a retaining fee employees must undertake to "
      "return to work at the end of the period during which the schools are closed."
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      "The retaining fee is earnings because it derived from a gainful occupation and is payable under the terms "
      "of the contract of employment. Further, the fee although paid in two instalments, was a payment in "
      "respect of the whole period of the six weeks holiday, and for the purpose of fixing the rate of earnings "
      "must be spread evenly over the period."
    H4 (block):
       /Placement /Block

      Span (inline)
        "Allocation of payments"
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.15
      "15149 If it is decided that the dependant has earnings in a holiday period comprised partly of holiday pay "
      "and partly of a special payment and the employment authority has not related the payments to specific "
      "weeks in the period, the holiday pay should ordinarily be allocated to the first week(s) of the holiday and "
      "the special payment to the remaining weeks. However, where it would be to a claimant’s advantage to "
      "attribute the payments in some different way, this should be done."
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      Span (inline)
        "Example "
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      "For the 6 week period of the summer holiday, a school meals assistant is to be paid holiday pay of £60 a "
      "week for 2 weeks plus a special payment of £80, all of which is regarded as earnings. Her husband falls "
      "sick at the beginning of the second week of the holiday period and claims Incapacity Benefit, including an "
      "increase for his wife. He remains sick for 4 weeks."
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      "His wife should be treated as receiving £60 holiday pay for the first week of the holiday and £20 special "
      "pay for each of the 4 succeeding weeks. By attributing the payments in this way, the claimant would be "
      "entitled to an increase for his wife throughout the period of his incapacity."
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      "If his incapacity lasted for at least 5 weeks, no increase would be payable for the fifth week because the "
      "remaining holiday pay (£60) would necessarily be attributed to it."
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      "Allocation of payments should not be made to weeks where no benefit considerations arise, for example "
      "to weeks occurring after a claimant has again become fit for work."

    P (block):

      "15285 Occupational or personal pension, or payments for redundancy which are paid as a lump-sum or "
      "are not related to a specific period should not be taken into account. Where a person has commuted "
      "periodic payments to a lump-sum this payment will not be taken into account. The payment was made at "
      "a point in time and was not therefore attributable to any period nor was it a periodical payment because a "
      "single payment cannot fall within the normal everyday meaning of “periodical”"
      Span (inline):
         /BaselineShift 0.099

       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.533
      Span (inline)
        "Calculation of the average weekly child care charge "
    H4 (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.411
      Span (inline)
        "Child care charges paid weekly"
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.15
      "15810 Where child care charges are paid weekly, the average weekly charge shall be calculated as "
      "follows"
      Span (inline):
         /BaselineShift 0.099
        "1"
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.41
      Span (inline)
        "1."
      " add together the child care charges from the most recent four full school term-time weeks and divide "
      "the result by four"
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      Span (inline)
        "Note:"
      " “School term-time” means the school term-time applicable to the child being cared for. The "
      "number of term-time weeks in a year should be treated as 39 and non term-time weeks as 13."
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      Span (inline)
        "2."
      " multiply the answer at "
      Span (inline)
        "1."
      " by 39. This figure becomes amount A (average weekly school term-time "
      "charge)"
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      Span (inline)
        "3. "
      "add together the child care charges from the most recent 2 out of school term-time weeks and divide "
      "the result by 2"
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
      Span (inline)
        "4. "
      "multiply the answer at "
      Span (inline)
        "3."
      " by 13. This figure becomes amount B (average weekly out of school term-"
      "time charge)"
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      Span (inline)
        "5. "
      "then apply the formula A + B ÷ 52 = average weekly child care charges"
      Span (inline):
         /BaselineShift 0.099
        "1"
      " (result rounded up to a whole "
      "penny)"
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      Span (inline)
        "6. "
      "deduct the amount at "
      Span (inline)
        "5."
      " (up to a maximum of £60) from the net weekly earnings."
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
       /TextAlign /End
      "1 SS Ben (C of E) Regs, Sch 2, para 4"
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block

      Span (inline)
        "Example "
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      Span (inline)
        "1."
      " Four full term-time weeks are £30, £30, £25, £30"
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      "Total = £115 ÷ 4 = £28.75"
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      Span (inline)
        "2."
      " A = £28.75 x 39 = £1121.25"
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      Span (inline)
        "3."
      " Two out of term-time weeks £50, £50. Total = £100 ÷ 2 = £50"
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      Span (inline)
        "4."
      " B = £50 x 13 = £650"
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      Span (inline)
        "5."
      " A (£1121.25) + B (£650) ÷ 52 = £34.07 average weekly child care charges"

        "INTRODUCTION"
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      "1 This Annex gives guidance about a Commissioner’s decision which radically altered the way in which "
      "DMs were to calculate earnings from S/E where the person concerned is providing BL accommodation "
      "on a commercial basis "
      Span (inline)
        "in their own home"
      ". The decision also affected the treatment of payments from "

    P (block):

    P (block):

       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      Span (inline)
        "What the Commissioner decided"
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      "5 The Commissioner held that three sums must be deducted when calculating earnings from providing "
      "BL accommodation in the claimants own home."
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      Span (inline)
        "1. "
      "For each boarder, the first £20 of the payments made, plus 50% of the remainder, must be excluded "
      "from the gross earnings."
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      Span (inline)
        "2. "
      "All expenses wholly and exclusively defrayed in the assessment period for the purposes of the "
      "employment must be deducted from the gross receipts in the process of calculating the net profit."
    P (block):
       /Placement /Block
       /SpaceBefore 0.393
      Span (inline)
        "3. "
      "For each boarder, the first £20 of the payments made, plus 50% of the remainder, must "
      Span (inline)
        "again"
      " be "
      "deducted, this time from the net earnings."
    P (block):

      "12 If a claimant receives payments by way of rent from a sub-tenant, the DM should first decide whether "
